使用前向链早期检测井里汶县 Beber 社区卫生中心孕妇的乙型肝炎风险

The Use of Forward Chaining for Early Detection of Hepatitis B Risk Among Pregnant Women at Beber Community Health Center, Cirebon Regency
作者： Yanto Haryanto, Maula Ismail
摘要中文翻译传播 (HBV) 感染仍然是印度尼西亚的一个主要公共卫生问题,特别是在孕妇中,延迟检测可能会增加母婴 传播 以及肝硬化和肝细胞癌等严重并发症的风险。实验室测试的高成本可能会导致 诊断 和 治疗 的延误。本研究旨在使用前向链接方法设计基于网络的孕妇乙型肝炎早期检测系统。采用研究与开发 (R&D) 设计的混合方法来开发和评估该系统。所开发的系统在 30 名受访者身上进行了测试,黑盒测试的成功率达到 100%。验证结果显示敏感性为 100%、特异性为 90%、阳性预测值 (PPV) 为 82%、阴性预测值 (NPV) 为 100%、准确度为 93%,表明诊断性能良好。开发的系统可以帮助孕妇早期检测乙型肝炎,并可能支持在初级医疗机构中早期识别母婴 传播 风险。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
传播 (HBV) 感染仍然是印度尼西亚的一个主要公共卫生问题,特别是在孕妇中,延迟检测可能会增加母婴 传播 以及肝硬化和肝细胞癌等严重并发症的风险。
设计与对象
采用研究与开发 (R&D) 设计的混合方法来开发和评估该系统。
核心方法
本研究旨在使用前向链接方法设计基于网络的孕妇乙型肝炎早期检测系统。
主要结果
所开发的系统在 30 名受访者身上进行了测试,黑盒测试的成功率达到 100%。验证结果显示敏感性为 100%、特异性为 90%、阳性预测值 (PPV) 为 82%、阴性预测值 (NPV) 为 100%、准确度为 93%,表明诊断性能良好。
解释与创新
开发的系统可以帮助孕妇早期检测乙型肝炎,并可能支持在初级医疗机构中早期识别母婴 传播 风险。
科研与公卫意义
该系统可能有助于解决导致 诊断 和 治疗 延迟的实验室检测成本高昂的问题,从而有可能改善孕妇乙型肝炎的早期干预。
局限与证据强度
该研究的样本量较小,只有 30 名受访者,这可能会限制普遍性。然而,高诊断性能指标表明该系统在此特定设置中的有效性具有中等证据强度。

The Use of Forward Chaining for Early Detection of Hepatitis B Risk Among Pregnant Women at Beber Community Health Center, Cirebon Regency

Authors: Yanto Haryanto, Maula Ismail
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection remains a major public health problem in Indonesia, particularly among pregnant women, where delayed detection may increase the risk of mother-to-child transmission as well as serious complications such as cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma. The high cost of laboratory testing may contribute to delays in diagnosis and treatment. This study aimed to design a web-based early detection system for hepatitis B in pregnant women using the Forward Chaining method. A mixed-methods approach with a Research and Development (R&D) design was employed to develop and evaluate the system. The developed system was tested on 30 respondents and achieved a 100% success rate in black-box testing. Validation results showed a sensitivity of 100%, specificity of 90%, positive predictive value (PPV) of 82%, negative predictive value (NPV) of 100%, and an accuracy of 93%, indicating good diagnostic performance. The developed system can assist in the early detection of hepatitis B among pregnant women and may support earlier identification of the risk of mother-to-child transmission in primary healthcare settings.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection remains a major public health problem in Indonesia, particularly among pregnant women, where delayed detection may increase the risk of mother-to-child transmission as well as serious complications such as cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma.
Design and population
A mixed-methods approach with a Research and Development (R&D) design was employed to develop and evaluate the system.
Core methods
This study aimed to design a web-based early detection system for hepatitis B in pregnant women using the Forward Chaining method.
Main results
The developed system was tested on 30 respondents and achieved a 100% success rate in black-box testing. Validation results showed a sensitivity of 100%, specificity of 90%, positive predictive value (PPV) of 82%, negative predictive value (NPV) of 100%, and an accuracy of 93%, indicating good diagnostic performance.
Interpretation and novelty
The developed system can assist in the early detection of hepatitis B among pregnant women and may support earlier identification of the risk of mother-to-child transmission in primary healthcare settings.
Scientific and public-health significance
The system may help address the high cost of laboratory testing that contributes to delays in diagnosis and treatment, potentially improving early intervention for HBV in pregnant women.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was conducted with a small sample size of 30 respondents, which may limit generalizability. However, the high diagnostic performance metrics suggest moderate evidence strength for the system's effectiveness in this specific setting.

预测功能性治愈和慢性乙型肝炎的病毒标记的研究进展

Advances in Viral Markers for Predicting Functional Cure of Chronic Hepatitis B
作者： Xinrong Lu, Xin Yang, Qishui Ou, Zhen Xun
摘要中文翻译摘要 慢性乙型肝炎 的一线 抗病毒药物 疗法包括核苷(酸)类似物和聚乙二醇化干扰素-α。然而,这两种疗法都不能完全消除肝细胞共价闭合环状DNA,因此目前无法实现完全治愈。功能性治愈,主要定义为持续乙型肝炎表面抗原丢失,是 慢性乙型肝炎 的最佳治疗目标。尽管联合或序贯核苷类似物/聚乙二醇干扰素-α治疗方案可提高乙型肝炎表面抗原血清清除率,但只有少数患者达到这一结果。因此,使用标准临床测定对 乙型肝炎病毒 病毒标记物进行量化对于在 治疗 之前和治疗监测期间预测 功能性治愈 至关重要。及早识别可能获得乙型肝炎表面抗原的患者可能有助于提高治愈率并可能降低医疗费用。本综述总结了慢性乙型肝炎中预测功能性治愈的病毒标记物的最新进展,评估了单个标记物的预测性能,并为优化临床治疗策略提供了证据。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染,特别是 慢性乙型肝炎,以及使用病毒标记来预测 功能性治愈。核心问题是如何通过评估单个病毒标志物的预测性能来优化 慢性乙型肝炎 的临床 治疗 策略。该综述不报告数据库、研究计数、汇总估计或建议。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对用于预测 慢性乙型肝炎 中的 功能性治愈 的病毒标记的最新进展的叙述性综合。它评估单个标志物的预测性能,并为优化临床 治疗 策略提供证据。该综述不报告综述类型、数据库、检索日期、资格标准、选择方法、纳入研究计数、荟萃分析或共识过程。
共识与结论
综述得出的结论是,功能性治愈(定义为乙型肝炎表面抗原持续丧失)是 慢性乙型肝炎 的最佳治疗目标。它还指出,联合或序贯核苷类似物/聚乙二醇化干扰素-α 方案可提高乙型肝炎表面抗原血清清除率,但只有少数患者达到这一结果。该审查不报告汇总估计或稳定结论。
争议与缺口
该综述强调,一线 抗病毒药物 疗法和联合疗法都无法完全消除肝细胞共价闭合环状 DNA,目前无法实现彻底治愈。它还指出,只有少数患者达到乙型肝炎表面抗原血清清除。该综述不报告异质性、相互矛盾的发现、薄弱的方法、缺失的人群或区域或未解决的机制。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,使用标准临床测定对 乙型肝炎病毒 病毒标记物进行量化对于在 治疗 之前和治疗监测期间预测 功能性治愈 至关重要。它还指出,早期识别可能实现乙型肝炎表面抗原丧失的患者可能有助于提高治愈率并可能降低医疗费用。该审查不报告未来研究、监测、诊断、治疗、疫苗接种、生态、风险评估或政策的优先事项。

Advances in Viral Markers for Predicting Functional Cure of Chronic Hepatitis B

Authors: Xinrong Lu, Xin Yang, Qishui Ou, Zhen Xun
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT First‐line antiviral therapies for chronic hepatitis B include nucleos(t)ide analogues and pegylated interferon‐α. However, neither therapy can completely eliminate hepatocellular covalently closed circular DNA, making complete cure currently unattainable. Functional cure, primarily defined as sustained hepatitis B surface antigen loss, is the optimal therapeuticgoal for chronic hepatitis B. Although combined or sequential nucleos(t)ide analogues/pegylated interferon‐α regimens improve hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance rates, only a minority of patients achieve this outcome. Therefore, quantification of hepatitis B virus viral markers using standard clinical assays is essential for predicting functional cure before treatment and during therapeutic monitoring. Early identification of patients likely to achieve hepatitis B surface antigen may help loss improve cure rates and potentially reduce medical costs. This review summarizes recent advances in viral markers for predicting functional cure in chronic hepatitis B, evaluates the predictive performance of individual markers, and provides evidence to optimize clinical treatment strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, specifically chronic hepatitis B, and the use of viral markers to predict a functional cure. The central question is how to optimize clinical treatment strategies for chronic hepatitis B by evaluating the predictive performance of individual viral markers. The review does not report on databases, study counts, pooled estimates, or recommendations.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of recent advances in viral markers for predicting functional cure in chronic hepatitis B. It evaluates the predictive performance of individual markers and provides evidence to optimize clinical treatment strategies. The review does not report on the type of review, databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, selection method, included-study count, meta-analysis, or consensus process.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that functional cure, defined as sustained hepatitis B surface antigen loss, is the optimal therapeutic goal for chronic hepatitis B. It also notes that combined or sequential nucleos(t)ide analogues/pegylated interferon-α regimens improve hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance rates, but only a minority of patients achieve this outcome. The review does not report on pooled estimates or stable conclusions.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that neither first-line antiviral therapies nor combined regimens can completely eliminate hepatocellular covalently closed circular DNA, making a complete cure currently unattainable. It also notes that only a minority of patients achieve hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance. The review does not report on heterogeneity, conflicting findings, weak methods, missing populations or regions, or unresolved mechanisms.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that quantification of hepatitis B virus viral markers using standard clinical assays is essential for predicting functional cure before treatment and during therapeutic monitoring. It also notes that early identification of patients likely to achieve hepatitis B surface antigen loss may help improve cure rates and potentially reduce medical costs. The review does not report on future priorities for research, surveillance, diagnosis, treatment, vaccination, ecology, risk assessment, or policy.

隐匿性 乙型肝炎病毒 感染的免疫生态学:遗传重塑和免疫介导的微观世界

The immunoecology of occult hepatitis B virus infection: genetic remodeling and the immune-mediated microcosm
作者： Feng Wang, Le Wang, Zhiguo Xu, Zequn Ou, Yun Wang, Haiying Yang, Jingxian Fei, Jingxian Song, Yizhu Chen, Ke Lv
摘要中文翻译隐匿性乙型肝炎感染 (OBI) 的特点是在没有可检测到的乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 的情况下,具有复制能力的 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) DNA 持续存在。尽管通常临床上无症状,但 OBI 仍然与输血传播感染、免疫抑制期间病毒再激活以及肝细胞癌 (HCC) 风险增加有关。这种持续的低复制状态背后的机制仍不完全清楚。在这篇综述中,我们总结了支持 OBI 通过病毒遗传适应、宿主 遗传和表观遗传调控以及肝内免疫微环境之间的动态相互作用来维持这一概念的当前证据。基于这些观察,我们提出免疫介导的微观世界作为概念框架,描述这些过程如何相互作用以维持长期免疫平衡。我们进一步讨论了协调病毒和 宿主 遗传重塑、免疫代谢调节和免疫检查点信号传导的新证据,同时明确区分 OBI 中直接证据支持的机制与从 慢性乙型肝炎、肝细胞癌或实验模型推断的机制。最后,我们研究了该框架对生物标志物发现、风险分层和治疗开发的影响,同时强调了当前证据的局限性以及在人类 OBI 中直接验证的需要。该框架不是提供明确的机制模型,而是旨在促进机制研究、生成可通过实验检验的假设,并帮助确定 OBI 发病机制 未来研究的优先事项。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注隐匿性 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染 (OBI),其特征是具有复制能力的 HBV DNA 持续存在,但未检测到乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg)。核心问题是通过病毒遗传适应、宿主 遗传和表观遗传调控以及肝内免疫微环境之间的相互作用来了解这种持续低复制状态的机制。
证据基础与方法
该评论是对 OBI 现有证据的叙述性综合。它不报告具体的数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究计数。该方法包括根据现有观察提出一个概念框架(免疫介导的微观世界),并将 OBI 中直接证据支持的机制与从相关条件或实验模型推断的机制区分开来。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,OBI 是通过病毒遗传适应、宿主 遗传和表观遗传调控以及肝内免疫微环境之间的动态相互作用来维持的。它提出免疫介导的微观世界作为一个框架来描述这些过程如何相互作用以维持长期的免疫平衡。达成一致的关键领域包括协调病毒和 宿主 遗传重塑、免疫代谢调节和 OBI 发病机制 中免疫检查点信号传导的作用。
争议与缺口
审查强调,OBI 的潜在机制仍未完全被了解。它强调需要区分 OBI 证据直接支持的机制和从 慢性乙型肝炎、肝细胞癌或实验模型推断的机制。目前的证据局限性包括许多提出的机制缺乏人类 OBI 的直接验证,并且该框架是作为调查的促进者而不是确定的模型提出的。
科研与实践启示
该框架的意义包括促进 OBI 的生物标志物发现、风险分层和治疗开发。该审查确定了 OBI 发病机制 未来研究的优先事项,强调需要在人类 OBI 中进行直接验证。该框架旨在生成可通过实验检验的假设并指导机制研究,而不是提供明确的指导。

The immunoecology of occult hepatitis B virus infection: genetic remodeling and the immune-mediated microcosm

Authors: Feng Wang, Le Wang, Zhiguo Xu, Zequn Ou, Yun Wang, Haiying Yang, Jingxian Fei, Jingxian Song, Yizhu Chen, Ke Lv
Original abstract (English)
Occult hepatitis B infection (OBI) is characterized by the persistence of replication-competent hepatitis B virus (HBV) DNA in the absence of detectable hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg). Although typically clinically silent, OBI remains associated with transfusion-transmitted infection, viral reactivation during immunosuppression, and an increased risk of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). The mechanisms underlying this persistent low-replicative state remain incompletely understood. In this review, we summarize current evidence supporting the concept that OBI is maintained through dynamic interactions among viral genetic adaptation, host genetic and epigenetic regulation, and the intrahepatic immune microenvironment. Building upon these observations, we propose the immune-mediated microcosm as a conceptual framework describing how these processes may interact to sustain long-term immune equilibrium. We further discuss emerging evidence for coordinated viral and host genetic remodeling, immunometabolic regulation, and immune checkpoint signaling, while explicitly distinguishing mechanisms supported by direct evidence in OBI from those inferred from chronic hepatitis B, hepatocellular carcinoma, or experimental models. Finally, we examine the implications of this framework for biomarker discovery, risk stratification, and therapeutic development, while emphasizing the current evidential limitations and the need for direct validation in human OBI. Rather than providing a definitive mechanistic model, this framework is intended to facilitate mechanistic investigation, generate experimentally testable hypotheses, and help identify priorities for future research into OBI pathogenesis.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on occult hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection (OBI), characterized by the persistence of replication-competent HBV DNA without detectable hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg). The central question is to understand the mechanisms underlying this persistent low-replicative state through interactions among viral genetic adaptation, host genetic and epigenetic regulation, and the intrahepatic immune microenvironment.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis summarizing current evidence on OBI. It does not report specific databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study counts. The approach involves proposing a conceptual framework (immune-mediated microcosm) based on existing observations and distinguishing mechanisms supported by direct evidence in OBI from those inferred from related conditions or experimental models.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that OBI is maintained through dynamic interactions among viral genetic adaptation, host genetic and epigenetic regulation, and the intrahepatic immune microenvironment. It proposes the immune-mediated microcosm as a framework to describe how these processes interact to sustain long-term immune equilibrium. Key areas of agreement include the role of coordinated viral and host genetic remodeling, immunometabolic regulation, and immune checkpoint signaling in OBI pathogenesis.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that the mechanisms underlying OBI remain incompletely understood. It emphasizes the need to distinguish between mechanisms directly supported by evidence in OBI and those inferred from chronic hepatitis B, hepatocellular carcinoma, or experimental models. Current evidential limitations include the lack of direct validation in human OBI for many proposed mechanisms, and the framework is presented as a facilitator for investigation rather than a definitive model.
Research and practice implications
The implications of the framework include facilitating biomarker discovery, risk stratification, and therapeutic development for OBI. The review identifies priorities for future research into OBI pathogenesis, emphasizing the need for direct validation in human OBI. The framework is intended to generate experimentally testable hypotheses and guide mechanistic investigation rather than providing definitive guidance.

探索 ABO 血型在 乙型肝炎病毒 感染中的作用:系统评价

Exploring the role of the ABO blood group types in hepatitis B virus infection: a systematic review
作者： Francisca Varpit, Donna Rudd, Victor J. Temple, Bruce Gummow
摘要中文翻译简介 ABO 血型与多种疾病有关,包括癌症、消化性溃疡、凝血病和感染。然而,这种关联的机制尚不清楚。本综述探讨了 ABO 血型在 HBV 感染中的作用。方法 使用人口、干预/暴露、比较器、结果框架对七个电子数据库进行系统检索,包括 1974 年至 2024 年间发表的所有研究。该综述在 PROSPERO 中注册(ID:CRD420251243615)。结果 来自四大洲 17 个国家的 35 篇文章被选中进行审查。其中 12 项研究表明 ABO 血型与 HBV 感染之间存在显着关联,其中大多数研究都是针对健康献血者。慢性乙型肝炎 感染者的肝纤维化和肝硬化疾病进展与 O 型和 A 型血有关。结论 将 ABO 血型与 HBV 联系起来的各种证据强调了其他因素的作用。ABO 血型与 HBV 基因型之间的联系仍不清楚。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述调查了人类 ABO 血型是否影响人类 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染的易感性或进展,重点关注 ABO 血型是否与 HBV 感染或疾病结果相关这一核心问题。
证据基础与方法
这是在 PROSPERO (CRD420251243615) 中注册的一项系统综述,使用人口、干预/暴露、比较器、结果框架检索了 7 个电子数据库,查找 1974 年至 2024 年间发表的研究。来自四大洲 17 个国家的 35 篇文章入选。
共识与结论
十二项研究报告了 ABO 血型与 HBV 感染之间的显着关联,其中大多数研究是在健康献血者中进行的。慢性乙型肝炎 疾病进展为肝纤维化和肝硬化与 O 型和 A 型血相关。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了将 ABO 血型与 HBV 感染联系起来的不同证据,并指出 ABO 血型与 HBV 基因型之间的联系仍不清楚。任何关联背后的机制尚未建立。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步的研究来阐明 ABO 血型与 HBV 感染和疾病进展之间的生物学机制,特别是关于 HBV 基因型和纤维化和肝硬化等长期结果。

Exploring the role of the ABO blood group types in hepatitis B virus infection: a systematic review

Authors: Francisca Varpit, Donna Rudd, Victor J. Temple, Bruce Gummow
Original abstract (English)
Introduction ABO blood groups have been associated with several disorders, including cancer, peptic ulcers, coagulopathy and infections. However, the mechanism of this association is unclear. This review examines the role ABO blood groups play in HBV infection. Methods A systematic search of seven electronic databases was conducted using the Population, Intervention/Exposure, Comparator, Outcome framework, including all studies published between 1974 and 2024. The review is registered in PROSPERO (ID: CRD420251243615). Results Thirty-five articles from seventeen countries across four continents were selected for review. Twelve of these demonstrated significant associations between ABO blood groups and HBV infection, with the majority investigating healthy blood donors. Disease progression to hepatic fibrosis and cirrhosis in individuals with chronic hepatitis B infection was associated with blood groups O and A. Conclusions Variable evidence linking ABO blood groups to HBV highlights the role of other factors. Links between ABO blood groups and HBV genotypes remain unclear.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review investigates whether human ABO blood group types influence susceptibility to or progression of hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in humans, focusing on the central question of whether ABO blood group is associated with HBV infection or disease outcomes.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic review registered in PROSPERO (CRD420251243615) that searched seven electronic databases for studies published between 1974 and 2024 using the Population, Intervention/Exposure, Comparator, Outcome framework. Thirty-five articles from seventeen countries across four continents were selected.
Consensus and conclusions
Twelve studies reported significant associations between ABO blood groups and HBV infection, with most studies conducted among healthy blood donors. Disease progression to hepatic fibrosis and cirrhosis in chronic hepatitis B was associated with blood groups O and A.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights variable evidence linking ABO blood groups to HBV infection and notes that links between ABO blood groups and HBV genotypes remain unclear. The mechanism underlying any association is not established.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to clarify the biological mechanisms linking ABO blood groups to HBV infection and disease progression, particularly regarding HBV genotypes and long-term outcomes such as fibrosis and cirrhosis.

乙型肝炎病毒 C基因型高危表型的分子基础及临床意义

Molecular Basis and Clinical Significance of the High-risk Phenotype of Hepatitis B Virus Genotype C
作者： Chenchen Huang, Zhongjian Liu, Jingyao Zhang, Tao Shen, Lei Sang
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 基因型 C 在东亚很常见,与不良预后相关,特别是肝硬化和肝细胞癌 (HCC)。临床上,C 基因型感染通常与持续的病毒复制、较晚的 HBeAg 血清转换、更活跃的肝脏炎症以及 HCC 风险增加有关。目前的证据表明,这些特征是由几个关键分子事件驱动的,包括基础核心启动子中的 A1762T/G1764A 双 突变、前核心区域中的 G1896A 突变、异常的 乙型肝炎病毒 X 蛋白功能和病毒整合。这些变化共同可能重塑病毒转录、抗原表达、宿主 免疫相互作用和致癌信号传导,从而促进疾病进展和肝癌发生。其他因素,如表观遗传变化、DNA损伤反应失调、肿瘤蛋白p53功能受损和自噬破坏等也可能参与其中,尽管它们的确切作用仍不清楚。值得注意的是,即使在使用有效的核苷(酸)类似物有效抑制病毒后,C 基因型患者仍可能具有相对较高的 HCC 残留风险。本文综述了C型HBV的分子病毒学特征、致病机制、免疫失调和临床意义,并讨论了基因型信息在风险分层、长期监测和慢性乙型肝炎临床评估中的潜在价值。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 乙型肝炎病毒 基因型 C、其分子基础、临床意义以及与东亚人群肝硬化和肝细胞癌 (HCC) 等不良预后的关联。
证据基础与方法
该综述总结了C基因型HBV的分子病毒学特征、致病机制、免疫失调和临床意义,并利用现有证据讨论了其在疾病进展和肝癌发生中的作用。
共识与结论
C 基因型感染与持续的病毒复制、较晚的 HBeAg 血清转换、更活跃的肝脏炎症以及 HCC 风险增加有关,这些因素是由 A1762T/G1764A 和 G1896A 突变 等分子事件、HBx 蛋白功能异常和病毒整合驱动的。
争议与缺口
C 基因型 发病机制 中表观遗传变化、DNA 损伤反应失调、p53 功能受损和自噬破坏的确切作用仍不清楚,尽管有效抑制了病毒,但残留的 HCC 风险仍然存在。
科研与实践启示
基因型信息可能对风险分层、长期 监测 和 慢性乙型肝炎 的临床评估有价值,特别是对于基因型 C 的患者。

Molecular Basis and Clinical Significance of the High-risk Phenotype of Hepatitis B Virus Genotype C

Authors: Chenchen Huang, Zhongjian Liu, Jingyao Zhang, Tao Shen, Lei Sang
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B virus (HBV) genotype C is common in East Asia and is associated with poor outcomes, particularly liver cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). Clinically, genotype C infection is generally associated with persistent viral replication, later HBeAg seroconversion, more active hepatic inflammation, and an increased risk of HCC. Current evidence suggests that these features are driven by several key molecular events, including the A1762T/G1764A double mutation in the basal core promoter, the G1896A mutation in the precore region, abnormal hepatitis B virus X protein function, and viral integration. Together, these changes may reshape viral transcription, antigen expression, host immune interactions, and oncogenic signaling, thereby contributing to disease progression and hepatocarcinogenesis. Other factors, such as epigenetic changes, dysregulated DNA damage responses, impaired tumor protein p53 function, and disrupted autophagy, may also be involved, although their exact roles remain unclear. Notably, even after effective viral suppression with potent nucleos(t)ide analogs, patients with genotype C may still have a relatively high residual risk of HCC. This review summarizes the molecular virological features, pathogenic mechanisms, immune dysregulation, and clinical significance of HBV genotype C, and discusses the potential value of genotype information in risk stratification, long-term surveillance, and clinical assessment of chronic hepatitis B.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on Hepatitis B virus genotype C, its molecular basis, clinical significance, and association with poor outcomes such as liver cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) in East Asian populations.
Evidence base and method
The review summarizes molecular virological features, pathogenic mechanisms, immune dysregulation, and clinical significance of HBV genotype C, drawing on existing evidence to discuss its role in disease progression and hepatocarcinogenesis.
Consensus and conclusions
Genotype C infection is associated with persistent viral replication, later HBeAg seroconversion, more active hepatic inflammation, and increased HCC risk, driven by molecular events like A1762T/G1764A and G1896A mutations, abnormal HBx protein function, and viral integration.
Controversies and gaps
The exact roles of epigenetic changes, dysregulated DNA damage responses, impaired p53 function, and disrupted autophagy in genotype C pathogenesis remain unclear, and residual HCC risk persists despite effective viral suppression.
Research and practice implications
Genotype information may have value in risk stratification, long-term surveillance, and clinical assessment of chronic hepatitis B, particularly for patients with genotype C.

乙型和丙型肝炎患者性问题的评估

The Evaluation of Sexual Problems in Patients With Hepatitis B and C
作者： Latife Merve YILDIZ, Onur ÖZTÜRK
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景病毒性肝炎是一个主要的全球健康问题,可导致慢性肝病、肝硬化和肝细胞癌,但其对社会心理和性的影响常常被忽视。乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎等慢性感染可能通过生物机制和与 传播 的耻辱和恐惧相关的情绪困扰对患者的性健康产生负面影响。尽管如此,关于肝炎 诊断 如何影响性行为和满意度的研究还很有限,特别是从初级保健的角度来看。本研究旨在调查乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎患者的 患病率 和性功能障碍的性质,并探讨相关的社会人口统计学和临床​​因素。方法 该研究采用观察分析设计。该人群包括接受 诊断 乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎感染随访的患者。对收集在密封信封中的患者进行了评估患者的社会人口学特征和性行为的调查问卷,以及 Golombok Rust 性满意度调查表。结果 132名患者参与;其中,82.6%(n = 109)和17.4%(n = 23)被诊断出患有乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎。诊断 后,90.2% (n = 119) 的参与者对感染伴侣感到焦虑,69.7% (n = 92) 的伴侣出现性问题的频率增加。阴道性交、肛交、接吻和抚摸的频率通常会减少;然而,口交和色情/色情流媒体的频率保持不变。而且,手淫的频率和保护需求大多增加。研究发现,阴道性交频率的下降与教育水平有关(p

范围与问题

The Evaluation of Sexual Problems in Patients With Hepatitis B and C

Authors: Latife Merve YILDIZ, Onur ÖZTÜRK
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Viral hepatitis is a major global health problem that can lead to chronic liver disease, cirrhosis, and hepatocellular carcinoma, but its psychosocial and sexual implications are often overlooked. Chronic infections such as hepatitis B and C may negatively affect patients’ sexual well-being through both biological mechanisms and emotional distress related to stigma and fear of transmission. Despite this, limited research has addressed how a hepatitis diagnosis influences sexual behavior and satisfaction, particularly from a primary care perspective. This study aimed to investigate the prevalence and nature of sexual dysfunction among individuals diagnosed with hepatitis B and C and to explore the associated sociodemographic and clinical factors. Methods The study had an observational, analytical design. The population comprised patients followed-up with diagnosis of hepatitis B and C infections. A questionnaire that evaluates the sociodemographic characteristics with sexual repertoire of the patients, and Golombok Rust Inventory of Sexual Satisfaction were applied to the patients collected in sealed envelopes. Results 132 patients participated; among whom, 82.6% (n = 109) and 17.4% (n = 23) were diagnosed with hepatitis B and C, respectively. After the diagnosis, 90.2% (n = 119) participants experienced anxiety about infecting their partners and 69.7% (n = 92) experienced an increase in the frequency of sexual problems of their partners. Frequency of vaginal sex, anal sex, kissing and touching usually decreased; however, the frequency of oral sex and erotic/porn streaming remained unchanged. Moreover, the frequency of masturbation and protection needs mostly increased. The decrease in the frequency of vaginal sex was found to be related to educational level (p
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study investigated the prevalence and nature of sexual dysfunction among individuals diagnosed with hepatitis B and C, exploring associated sociodemographic and clinical factors.
Evidence base and method
The study had an observational, analytical design with a population of patients diagnosed with hepatitis B and C infections. Data were collected using a questionnaire evaluating sociodemographic characteristics and sexual repertoire, along with the Golombok Rust Inventory of Sexual Satisfaction.
Consensus and conclusions
90.2% of participants experienced anxiety about infecting their partners, and 69.7% reported an increase in sexual problems among partners. The frequency of vaginal sex, anal sex, kissing, and touching decreased, while oral sex and erotic/porn streaming remained unchanged. Masturbation frequency and protection needs mostly increased.
Controversies and gaps
The study identified a decrease in vaginal sex frequency related to educational level, but the provided evidence does not report specific statistical details or p-values. Limited research exists on how hepatitis diagnosis influences sexual behavior and satisfaction from a primary care perspective.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to address the limited understanding of hepatitis's impact on sexual behavior and satisfaction, particularly from a primary care perspective. The findings highlight the need for interventions addressing sexual well-being and partner anxiety in hepatitis patients.

尼日利亚主要人群中的 患病率 肝炎:范围界定审查

Hepatitis Prevalence Among Key Populations in Nigeria: A Scoping Review
作者： Anthonia Chukwuemeka, Adamu Ishaku Akyala, O. Sanni, Judith Otieno, Lenny Gitundu, Gertrude Dzigbordi Agbeshie, Awayimbo Ruth Jaggu, Adaora Ejikeme, R. Amaza, Dr Judith Kose Otieno
摘要中文翻译尼日利亚面临着沉重的病毒性肝炎负担,其重点人群(KP)包括性工作者、男男性行为者(MSM)、跨性别者、注射吸毒者(PWID)和监狱人员。该研究旨在审查尼日利亚 KP 肝炎的现有证据,评估研究质量,突出知识差距并指导公共卫生政策。我们按照 Arksey 和 O’Malley 框架以及系统评价的首选报告项目和范围界定审查指南的元分析扩展进行了范围界定审查。检索了六个数据库和灰色文献,查找了尼日利亚 KP 中肝炎 患病率 的研究(2008-2025 年)。两名独立评审员筛选并提取数据,并使用改编后的乔安娜·布里格斯研究所标准评估研究质量。从筛选的 1,270 份记录中纳入了 25 项研究,涵盖尼日利亚 12 个州的 5 个 KP 团体。乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 患病率 的比例从埃努古性工作者的 4.0% 到监狱中的 23.0%,而阿布贾和拉各斯的 MSM 和跨性别者则为 10.0%。注射吸毒者的乙肝病毒感染率为 6.6-7.8%,丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 患病率 感染率为 2.3-3.3%,而监狱的负担最高。尽管 患病率 人口较多,但尼日利亚北部仍显示出重大数据差距。68% 的研究发现方法学上的缺陷,丙肝病毒 (32%)、变性人群 (12%) 和农村群体 (8%) 的证据有限。尼日利亚的 KP,特别是囚犯,面临着比一般人群更高的肝炎负担。HCV、跨性别群体和农村地区存在重大证据差距,凸显需要进行更多研究、更广泛的 监测 以及人类免疫缺陷病毒-肝炎综合服务。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围界定审查研究了尼日利亚关键人群 (KP) 中的 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 患病率,包括性工作者、男男性行为者 (MSM)、跨性别者、注射吸毒者 (PWID) 和监狱人员,以评估负担并确定知识差距。
证据基础与方法
根据 Arksey 和 O'Malley 框架和 PRISMA-ScR 指南进行了范围界定审查,检索了 2008 年至 2025 年的六个数据库和灰色文献。两名独立评审员筛选了 1,270 份记录,纳入了涵盖尼日利亚 12 个州的 5 个 KP 小组的 25 项研究,并使用改编后的乔安娜·布里格斯研究所标准评估了研究质量。
共识与结论
KP 中的 HBV 患病率 差异显着,从性工作者 (Enugu) 的 4.0% 到监狱中的 23.0%,而阿布贾和拉各斯的 MSM 和跨性别者则为 10.0%。注射吸毒者的 HBV 患病率 记录为 6.6–7.8%。KP,特别是囚犯,面临着比一般人群更高的肝炎负担。
争议与缺口
主要证据差距存在于尼日利亚北部、HCV 研究(占研究的 32%)、变性人群(12%)和农村群体(8%)。68% 的研究发现方法学上的缺陷,限制了研究结果的可靠性。
科研与实践启示
迫切需要对 HCV、跨性别人群和农村地区进行更多研究,同时扩大 监测 和综合艾滋病毒肝炎服务,以解决尼日利亚 KP 的不成比例的负担。

Hepatitis Prevalence Among Key Populations in Nigeria: A Scoping Review

Authors: Anthonia Chukwuemeka, Adamu Ishaku Akyala, O. Sanni, Judith Otieno, Lenny Gitundu, Gertrude Dzigbordi Agbeshie, Awayimbo Ruth Jaggu, Adaora Ejikeme, R. Amaza, Dr Judith Kose Otieno
Original abstract (English)
Nigeria faces a high burden of viral hepatitis, with key populations (KPs) such as sex workers, men who have sex with men (MSM), transgender people, people who inject drugs (PWIDs), and people in prisons. The study aims to review existing evidence on hepatitis among KPs in Nigeria, evaluate study quality, highlight knowledge gaps, and guide public health policy. We conducted a scoping review following the Arksey and O’Malley framework and Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses extension for Scoping Reviews guidelines. Six databases and gray literature were searched for studies on hepatitis prevalence among KPs in Nigeria (2008–2025). Two independent reviewers screened and extracted data, and study quality was assessed using adapted Joanna Briggs Institute criteria. From 1,270 records screened, 25 studies were included, covering five KP groups in 12 Nigerian states. Hepatitis B virus (HBV) prevalence ranged from 4.0% among sex workers in Enugu to 23.0% in prisons, with MSM and transgender people showing 10.0% in Abuja and Lagos. PWID recorded 6.6– 7.8% HBV and 2.3–3.3% hepatitis C virus (HCV) prevalence, while prisons carried the highest burden. Northern Nigeria showed major data gaps despite higher population prevalence. Methodological weaknesses were found in 68% of studies, with limited evidence for HCV (32%), transgender populations (12%), and rural groups (8%). KPs in Nigeria, particularly prisoners, face a higher hepatitis burden than the general population. There are major evidence gaps on HCV, transgender groups, and rural areas, highlighting the need for more research, wider surveillance, and integrated human immunodeficiency virus-hepatitis services.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review examines hepatitis B virus (HBV) prevalence among key populations (KPs) in Nigeria, including sex workers, men who have sex with men (MSM), transgender people, people who inject drugs (PWIDs), and people in prisons, to assess the burden and identify knowledge gaps.
Evidence base and method
A scoping review was conducted following the Arksey and O'Malley framework and PRISMA-ScR guidelines, searching six databases and gray literature from 2008–2025. Two independent reviewers screened 1,270 records, included 25 studies covering five KP groups in 12 Nigerian states, and assessed study quality using adapted Joanna Briggs Institute criteria.
Consensus and conclusions
HBV prevalence varied significantly among KPs, ranging from 4.0% in sex workers (Enugu) to 23.0% in prisons, with MSM and transgender people showing 10.0% in Abuja and Lagos. PWIDs recorded 6.6–7.8% HBV prevalence. KPs, particularly prisoners, face a higher hepatitis burden than the general population.
Controversies and gaps
Major evidence gaps exist in Northern Nigeria, HCV research (32% of studies), transgender populations (12%), and rural groups (8%). Methodological weaknesses were found in 68% of studies, limiting the reliability of findings.
Research and practice implications
There is an urgent need for more research on HCV, transgender populations, and rural areas, alongside expanded surveillance and integrated HIV-hepatitis services to address the disproportionate burden among KPs in Nigeria.

Optimizing Hepatitis B screening: A comparison of ELISA and rapid tests
作者： Vero Hanitriniaina Harijaona Vololontsoa, Anselme Rakotoarivelo, Andry Rasamindrakotroka, Ny Onja Niaina Raharijaona, E. Verenako

Optimizing Hepatitis B screening: A comparison of ELISA and rapid tests

Authors: Vero Hanitriniaina Harijaona Vololontsoa, Anselme Rakotoarivelo, Andry Rasamindrakotroka, Ny Onja Niaina Raharijaona, E. Verenako

Brazil seeks validation of the elimination of mother-to-child transmission of hepatitis B - Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)

A high priorityPublic-health news

Brazil seeks validation of the elimination of mother-to-child transmission of hepatitis B - Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)

News Brief
On July 27, 2026, Brazil requested validation from the Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) regarding the elimination of mother-to-child transmission of hepatitis B virus (HBV). The PAHO news release on July 29, 2026, highlighted this initiative as part of broader regional health efforts but did not confirm Brazil's success or provide specific metrics. The request aligns with PAHO's focus on hepatitis B elimination in the Americas, though the evidence lacks details on case numbers, transmission rates, or public health interventions. This development reflects ongoing efforts to eradicate HBV in vulnerable populations, particularly infants, but remains unverified without additional data. The initiative is part of PAHO's strategic goals for hepatitis B control, yet its status remains under review.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

2026-07 - 下一代技术瞄准乙型肝炎 - Wits 大学

2026-07 - Next-generation technologies take aim at hepatitis B - Wits University
新闻简报Wits University researchers are advancing next-generation technologies to combat hepatitis B virus (HBV), focusing on novel vaccines and treatments. Four scientists at the Antiviral Gene Therapy Research Unit (AGTRU) are developing self-amplifying RNA vaccines to reduce doses and costs, adenoviral-vector/mRNA boosters for broader protection, and microRNA mimics and CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing to target chronic infections. With 240 million chronic cases globally and 1.1 million annual deaths, Africa bears 68% of new infections. The research prioritizes high-risk groups like infants and children, addressing gaps in current vaccine coverage and non-responsiveness. These innovations aim to improve prevention and treatment in resource-limited settings.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 28 日,抗病毒药物 基因治疗研究单位 (AGTRU) 正在进行研究活动。
地点与对象
南非威特沃特斯兰德大学(Wits University)进行的研究重点关注非洲等高负担地区的人群,特别是婴儿和幼儿。
事件
据 Wits 大学报道,AGTRU 的四位年轻科学家正在开发用于 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 治疗 和预防的下一代技术,包括新型 疫苗 和基因疗法。
规模、影响与风险
In 2024, approximately 240 million people were living with chronic HBV, with 1.1 million deaths related to cirrhosis and liver cancer. Africa accounted for 68% of new chronic infections. The virus poses a higher risk when acquired in infancy or early childhood.
应对、状态与不确定性
研究正在进行中,项目使用 microRNA 模拟物和 CRISPR-Cas9 基因编辑来解决 疫苗 无反应、剂量减少和慢性 HBV 治疗 问题。南非自 1995 年起已将 HBV 疫苗 纳入其儿童免疫计划,但覆盖范围差距和无反应仍然是挑战。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

2026-07 - Next-generation technologies take aim at hepatitis B - Wits University

News Brief
Wits University researchers are advancing next-generation technologies to combat hepatitis B virus (HBV), focusing on novel vaccines and treatments. Four scientists at the Antiviral Gene Therapy Research Unit (AGTRU) are developing self-amplifying RNA vaccines to reduce doses and costs, adenoviral-vector/mRNA boosters for broader protection, and microRNA mimics and CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing to target chronic infections. With 240 million chronic cases globally and 1.1 million annual deaths, Africa bears 68% of new infections. The research prioritizes high-risk groups like infants and children, addressing gaps in current vaccine coverage and non-responsiveness. These innovations aim to improve prevention and treatment in resource-limited settings.
Original source text (English)
AboutPeople/StaffAlumniLibraryVisit WitsGiveApply Homepage Study at WitsStudentsFaculties and SchoolsTeaching and LearningResearch and InnovationNews Search Search the site Search Menu Search Search the site Search Start main page content HomeNewsLatest newsResearch news2026 2026-07 Next-generation technologies take aim at hepatitis B 28 July 2026 - Wits University Four young scientists at AGTRU leverage vaccine science and gene therapy advances for novel treatment and prevention of hepatitis B. The students' projects at the Antiviral Gene Therapy Research Unit (AGTRU) address hepatitis B virus (HBV) at different stages, from strengthening protection against infection to targeting it once it has become chronic. As World Hepatitis Day is marked on 28 July, these projects show why HBV must be tackled at every stage, from preventing infection to disabling the virus once it has entered liver cells. This work is most urgent for babies and young children, and in high-burden regions such as Africa. In 2024, around 240 million people were living with chronic HBV, while 1.1 million died from related cirrhosis and liver cancer. Africa accounted for 68% of new chronic infections. The virus is particularly dangerous when acquired in infancy or early childhood because babies and young children are far less likely than adults to clear it. HBV infection may remain silent for years while continuing to damage the liver, eventually causing fibrosis, cirrhosis, liver failure and liver cancer. South Africa incorporated the highly effective hepatitis B vaccine into its childhood immunisation programme in 1995. However, coverage gaps remain, while around 5–10% of vaccinated people do not produce a sufficient protective antibody response. A vaccine that could do more with less AGTRU PhD student Nazia Samudh is developing an HBV vaccine using self-amplifying RNA, one of the emerging generation of RNA vaccine platforms. Like conventional mRNA, it gives cells temporary instructions to produce a harmless viral antigen that trains the immune system. Self-amplifying RNA can briefly make additional copies of that message inside the cell. This results in more antigen being produced from a relatively lower vaccine dose. Because more antigen may be produced from a smaller starting dose, the platform could reduce the amount of RNA needed and, potentially, the cost of vaccination. “Some of our main goals are to address non-responsiveness to current HBV vaccines and investigate whether we can reduce the number of doses required,” says Samudh. Currently, three doses are required, and Samudh aims to elicit sufficient protection with just two doses. Fewer clinic visits could make vaccination easier in communities where distance, cost and inconsistent access to healthcare make it difficult to complete a multi-dose schedule. Combining platforms for broader protection PhD student Keila Neves is testing an adenoviral-vector vaccine followed by an mRNA booster. “It’s not just a vaccine candidate; it’s an entire regimen,” says Neves. Samudh and Neves are also testing vaccine candidates that present more of HBV’s outer surface to the immune system. Existing vaccines mainly use the small hepatitis B surface antigen; their candidates incorporate additional regions from the virus’s larger surface proteins, potentially stimulating a broader immune response. The candidates are designed around HBV subgenotype A1, which predominates in southern Africa and is associated with an increased risk of liver cancer. Silencing the virus AGTRU Master’s student Pieter Jonker is developing a potential treatment for people already living with chronic HBV. Once HBV has infected a liver cell, it produces messenger RNA carrying the instructions needed to make viral proteins and new virus particles. Jonker is developing microRNA mimics that recognise and bind to these viral messages. By prompting the cell to destroy the messenger RNA or preventing it from being read, the treatment could reduce viral protein production and suppress replication. “We are generating a mimic of this microRNA that will bind to the virus’s messenger RNA, essentially stopping it from making more viruses,” he explains. Gene editing and “mini livers” AGTRU PhD student Tasneem Farhad is investigating whether CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing can recognise and disable persistent HBV DNA in liver cells. A modified adenoviral vector would deliver the gene-editing machinery to its target. She is also developing a three-dimensional model of chronic HBV infection. Ordinary mice do not naturally reproduce human HBV infection well, making it difficult to test potential treatments. “The idea is to grow liver cells as a ‘mini liver’, infect them with hepatitis B and model what the infection looks like in a human,” says Farhad. Such models could provide a better bridge between conventional cell experiments and later-stage studies. Share 0 2 Close ShareThis Copy and PasteChat with me Kudubot Reply in 10 seconds. Location and population
Research conducted at the University of the Witwatersrand (Wits University) in South Africa, focusing on populations in high-burden regions such as Africa, particularly babies and young children.
Event
Four young scientists at AGTRU are developing next-generation technologies for hepatitis B virus (HBV) treatment and prevention, including novel vaccines and gene therapies, as reported by Wits University.
Scale, impact and risk
In 2024, approximately 240 million people were living with chronic HBV, with 1.1 million deaths related to cirrhosis and liver cancer. Africa accounted for 68% of new chronic infections. The virus poses a higher risk when acquired in infancy or early childhood.
Response, status and uncertainty
Research is ongoing, with projects addressing vaccine non-responsiveness, dose reduction, and chronic HBV treatment using microRNA mimics and CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing. South Africa has incorporated the HBV vaccine into its childhood immunization program since 1995, but coverage gaps and non-responsiveness remain challenges.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

TAF (Vemlidy) now listed for hepatitis B virus treatment on provincial and federal formularies in Canada - CATIE.ca

News Brief
CATIE.ca reports that tenofovir alafenamide (TAF, Vemlidy) is now listed for hepatitis B virus treatment on provincial and federal formularies across Canada. This policy change addresses a critical access issue, as many people with hepatitis B cannot afford the drug without private insurance. The listing of TAF, a recommended treatment for hepatitis B, represents a significant step in improving treatment accessibility for affected populations in Canada. The information is based on a full report from CATIE.ca, Canada's source for HIV and hepatitis C information.

Published on 2026-07-30. Event date not explicitly reported in the evidence.
Location and population
Affected population: People with hepatitis B virus infection.
Event
Tenofovir alafenamide (TAF, Vemlidy) has been listed for hepatitis B virus treatment on provincial and federal formularies in Canada. This is a confirmed policy change.
Scale, impact and risk
Cases: Not reported in the supplied evidence. Deaths: Not reported in the supplied evidence. Exposures: Not reported in the supplied evidence. The change improves access to treatment for people with hepatitis B, particularly those without private insurance.
Response, status and uncertainty
The provinces across Canada have agreed to subsidize TAF for people with hepatitis B. The source is a full original report body from CATIE.ca, a reputable provider of HIV and hepatitis C information in Canada.

Cary James 的初级保健如何缩小乙型肝炎检测差距 - Patient Care Online

How Primary Care Can Close the Hepatitis B Testing Gap, With Cary James - Patient Care Online
新闻简报本文讨论了乙型肝炎检测和认识方面持续存在的差距,特别是在 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 领域。一项由 GSK 资助、针对加拿大、日本、台湾、中国和德国约 5000 名成年人进行的调查显示,公众对慢性乙型肝炎的了解仍然非常有限。在加拿大,尽管慢性乙型肝炎占全球肝癌病例的 56%,但在接受调查的 1061 名成年人中,只有 31% 的人将慢性乙型肝炎视为高癌症风险。近 90% 的受访者不知道或对慢性乙型肝炎知之甚少,45% 的加拿大成年人表示他们从未接受过检测,因为他们没有考虑过这样做或不认为自己有风险。该文章强调了初级保健机构中慢性乙型肝炎检测正常化的重要性,类似于艾滋病毒和结核病检测,并提出了一些策略,例如在例行就诊期间提供检测、教育社区和减少耻辱感。世界肝炎联盟首席执行官卡里·詹姆斯 (Cary James) 强调需要开展宣传活动和常规检测来弥补这些差距,特别是在乙型肝炎资金不断减少的高收入国家。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-07-28。每年的 7 月 28 日是世界肝炎日,内容中有提及。
地点与对象
该调查在加拿大、日本、台湾、中国和德国进行,针对 18 至 54 岁及以上的成年人,重点关注 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 风险较高的人群。
事件
葛兰素史克 (GSK) 资助的一项调查发现,公众对 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 的了解有限,许多人不知道其癌症风险,也没有考虑进行检测。本文讨论了提高初级保健机构检测率的策略。
规模、影响与风险
在接受调查的 1061 名加拿大成年人中,只有 31% 的人将慢性乙型肝炎视为高癌症风险,尽管慢性乙型肝炎占全球肝癌病例的比例高达 56%。近 90% 的受访者不知道或对慢性乙型肝炎知之甚少,45% 的加拿大成年人表示他们从未接受过检测,因为他们没有考虑过这样做或不认为自己有风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文概述了初级保健解决检测差距的策略,包括实施协议、标准化检测和减少耻辱。它提到了在高收入国家开展宣传活动和常规测试的必要性,但没有提供具体的调查或测试结果。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

How Primary Care Can Close the Hepatitis B Testing Gap, With Cary James - Patient Care Online

News Brief
This article discusses the persistent gaps in hepatitis B testing and awareness, particularly in chronic hepatitis B (CHB). A survey funded by GSK, conducted among approximately 5000 adults in Canada, Japan, Taiwan, China, and Germany, revealed that public understanding of CHB remains strikingly limited. In Canada, only 31% of 1061 adults surveyed rated CHB as a high cancer risk, even though it accounts for up to 56% of liver cancer cases globally. Nearly 90% of respondents were unaware or knew very little about CHB, and 45% of Canadian adults said they have never been tested because they had not considered doing so or did not believe they were at risk. The article highlights the importance of normalizing CHB testing in primary care settings, similar to HIV and TB testing, and suggests strategies such as offering tests during routine visits, educating communities, and reducing stigma. Cary James, CEO of the World Hepatitis Alliance, emphasizes the need for awareness campaigns and routine testing to address these gaps, especially in high-income countries where funding for hepatitis B is decreasing.
Original source text (English)
As World Hepatitis Day highlights global testing gaps, an expert outlines how primary care can identify at-risk patients sooner. World Hepatitis Day falls each year on July 28, marking a global effort to close persistent gaps in hepatitis B diagnosis and care. A survey funded and commissioned by GSK, conducted online among approximately 5000 adults across Canada, Japan, Taiwan, China, and Germany, found that public understanding of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) remains strikingly limited. Among the 1061 Canadian adults aged 18 to 54 and older surveyed, only 31% rated CHB a high cancer risk, even though the virus accounts for up to 56% of liver cancer cases globally. Nearly 9 in 10 respondents reported being unaware or knowing very little about CHB, and 45% of Canadian adults said they have never been tested because they had not considered doing so or did not believe they were at risk. Among individuals considered at higher risk for CHB, nearly half said they do not intend to get tested, and separate findings show that 80% of at-risk individuals have never heard of CHB, with roughly 7 in 10 remaining untested. Misconceptions about prevention persist as well: 55% of Canadians incorrectly believe there is no treatment for CHB, and about 3 in 4 are unaware that vaccination offers protection. In the following Q&A, Cary James, CEO of World Hepatitis Alliance, discusses what this awareness gap means for how primary care clinicians should approach hepatitis B risk assessment and what practical steps can help normalize CHB testing during routine visits: Cary James: There's several ways in primary care to address this, right? You could have a protocol in place depending on how hepatitis B is manifested in that particular country. If there are certain populations that are more at risk, maybe certain migrant communities from high prevalence countries, first or second generation, or even key populations, you can put a protocol in if someone comes in for even a routine visit in primary care that they would be offered a hepatitis B test. This kind of comes back to whether those primary care physicians feel comfortable with doing that, or whether those people are familiar. It should just be routine. In most countries, hepatitis B tests are not that expensive, especially lab tests, so it should just be part of a general healthy living range of tests or things that are checked at least once in their life. In Germany, they have a policy that everyone should be tested at least once in their life. I think that is the protocol, and that would really make a huge difference. Sometimes, if you make an exception about something, then it makes it more scary. Streamlining it in primary care in the same way that they're talking about with HIV services in primary care. TB is talking about the same thing, like MDCV services. The main conversation about how we're going to solve the issues around decreased international funding in developing countries, but even in high income countries, is that things have to be evolved when they can in primary care, and hepatitis B is exactly the same. Cary James: I think it's kind of what I said before, right? If you know the communities in your catchment area of your clinic that may be affected by viral hepatitis, if you have some communities that are disproportionately affected, put some information in your waiting rooms. Talk to the patients about it during routine visits, and maybe put a protocol in place that all people from those communities are offered a test when they're also doing other kinds of routine tests. I think it's really important to normalize it as much as we can and take the stigma away from it. I think that once people know about it and know about the cancer risk, they want to get tested. They don't want to have something that they think might cause cancer. So, if you explain it in a way that's sort of like that, without prejudice, and this is just something that's important for you to know because no one wants to get cancer, then I think it's a much more appealing proposition than if you just talk about it as a singular concern. It is often associated with sexual transmission, which unfortunately still has lots of baggage on it around promiscuity and all kinds of stuff it doesn't deserve. I think the more you can normalize it and make the information available and make it part of the day-to-day care, the more applicants you'll get. Editors’ Note: Cary James is chief executive officer of the World Hepatitis Alliance. Response, status and uncertainty
The article outlines strategies for primary care to address the testing gap, including implementing protocols, normalizing testing, and reducing stigma. It references the need for awareness campaigns and routine testing in high-income countries, but no specific investigation or testing results are provided.

Bepirovirsen and the Push to Close Hepatitis B Care Gaps - Patient Care Online
新闻简报On World Hepatitis Day 2026, expert hepatologists discussed bepirovirsen, a promising new therapy for chronic hepatitis B (CHB) with a 19% functional cure rate in phase 3 trials, rising to 26% in a key subgroup. With over 240 million people affected globally and most cases undiagnosed or untreated, the drug's novel mechanism could drive renewed screening efforts. However, questions remain about care delivery: while many patients, especially in Asian communities, are managed in primary care, experts debate whether specialized centers are better equipped to handle monitoring demands like ALT flares. GSK has secured FDA Priority Review and Breakthrough Therapy designation, with an October 2026 decision date. The discussion highlights the need for education and adaptable care models to address persistent gaps in hepatitis B management.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于 2026 年 7 月 28 日。活动背景:世界肝炎日(7 月 28 日)和欧洲肝脏研究协会大会上公布的最新第 3 阶段数据,并于 2026 年 5 月 28 日发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》上。
地点与对象
Global, with emphasis on the United States and Asian communities. Affected population: Over 240 million people worldwide with chronic hepatitis B (CHB), including a subgroup of approximately 45% with baseline hepatitis B surface antigen levels of 1000 IU/mL or lower.
事件
讨论贝匹罗韦森,一种研究中的反义寡核苷酸,定位为 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 的潜在 功能性治愈。肝病专家(Nancy Reau,医学博士;Tatyana Kushner,医学博士;Kimberly Brown,医学博士)权衡了初级保健与专门中心以提供此 治疗。GSK 报告称,在 B-Well 1 和 B-Well 2 试验中,贝匹罗韦森接受者的 功能性治愈 汇总率为 19%,而基线乙型肝炎表面抗原水平为 1000 IU/mL 或更低的亚组中,这一比例为 26%。
规模、影响与风险
CHB affects over 240 million people worldwide and accounts for over half of global liver cancer cases. Bepirovirsen showed a pooled functional cure rate of 19% (233 of 1220) compared to 0% with placebo. The rate rose to 26% in a subgroup representing approximately 45% of diagnosed CHB cases. Despite progress, most CHB cases in the United States remain undiagnosed, and most diagnosed and treatment-eligible patients are not receiving therapy. The new drug's novel mechanism could drive renewed screening efforts.
应对、状态与不确定性
治疗 正在接受审查,PDUFA 目标行动日期为 2026 年 10 月 26 日。专家们正在讨论护理提供模式,考虑 治疗 是否应该在初级保健或专科中心实施,特别是对于亚洲社区。人们对监测需求表示担忧,包括针对 ALT 耀斑的 监测。葛兰素史克正在积极规划教育工作。具有新机制的新药的批准可能会导致筛查和护理标准的提高。然而,来源是完整的原始报告机构,分析基于专家讨论而不是官方政策。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bepirovirsen and the Push to Close Hepatitis B Care Gaps - Patient Care Online

News Brief
On World Hepatitis Day 2026, expert hepatologists discussed bepirovirsen, a promising new therapy for chronic hepatitis B (CHB) with a 19% functional cure rate in phase 3 trials, rising to 26% in a key subgroup. With over 240 million people affected globally and most cases undiagnosed or untreated, the drug's novel mechanism could drive renewed screening efforts. However, questions remain about care delivery: while many patients, especially in Asian communities, are managed in primary care, experts debate whether specialized centers are better equipped to handle monitoring demands like ALT flares. GSK has secured FDA Priority Review and Breakthrough Therapy designation, with an October 2026 decision date. The discussion highlights the need for education and adaptable care models to address persistent gaps in hepatitis B management.
Original source text (English)
This World Hepatitis Day, 3 expert hepatologists discuss bepirovirsen's functional cure data and weigh primary care against specialized centers for delivering this treatment. Nancy Reau, MD; Tatyana Kushner, MD; Kimberly Brown, MD World Hepatitis Day, observed July 28, arrives this year alongside new phase 3 data on bepirovirsen, an investigational antisense oligonucleotide positioned as a potential functional cure for chronic hepatitis B (CHB) .1 CHB affects more than 240 million people worldwide and accounts for over half of global liver cancer cases, underscoring the diagnostic and treatment gaps World Hepatitis Day is meant to spotlight.1 GSK recently reported a pooled functional cure rate of 19% among bepirovirsen recipients across the B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials (233 of 1220 vs 0 of 614 with placebo; P <.001 in both trials).Results were presented at the European Association for the Study of the Liver congress and published in the New England Journal of Medicine on May 28, 2026. The rate rose to 26% among participants with baseline hepatitis B surface antigen levels of 1000 IU/mL or lower, a subgroup representing approximately 45% of diagnosed CHB cases globally.1 The US Food and Drug Administration has granted the therapy Priority Review and Breakthrough Therapy designation, with a PDUFA target action date of October 26, 2026.1 Despite that progress, most CHB cases in the United States remain undiagnosed, and most patients who are diagnosed and treatment-eligible are not receiving therapy. A new drug with a novel mechanism could drive renewed screening efforts. It also raises questions about where treatment will be delivered, since many patients, particularly within Asian communities, are managed in primary care rather than hepatology practices. Liver Lineup: Bepirovirsen and the Push Toward Functional Cure in Hepatitis B On a recent episode of Liver Lineup: Updates & Unfiltered Insights , Tatyana Kushner, MD, joined hosts Nancy Reau, MD, and Kimberly Brown, MD, to discuss how care delivery models may need to adapt to accommodate bepirovirsen. Together, they weighed primary care providers' existing experience managing complex hepatitis B populations against the added monitoring demands, including surveillance for ALT flares, that come with a new mechanism of action. Kimberly Brown: Many patients with hepatitis B are not in our practices, right? They're in primary care practices, especially the Asian population. There are several primary care practitioners in the community that manage large groups of hepatitis B patients. Do you think that's an appropriate setting? That's where a lot of these patients are sitting, they're not really in hepatology practices always. What are we thinking about the uptake in those practices? Tatyana Kushner: That's a really good point, and I think probably the first step will have to be to increase education and awareness that this treatment is on the horizon, and I do think that GSK, the company that makes bepirovirsen, are really thinking actively about how to do that and how to target their educational efforts. So the first step, I think, will be to make everyone aware that it's on the horizon. Then, in terms of where this treatment is administered, again, it will depend on how it looks in terms of monitoring, but to me it seems more that there will be some specialized centers that are able to take this on and treat with this medication. Would it be a situation where those patients who are currently being followed in primary care actually have to be referred to a specialized center just to complete this treatment course, and then referred back to their primary care for ongoing treatment after? I'm just not convinced that with all the nuances with this treatment, that that will be able to be done in routine primary care settings, but I think we'll have to see. Nancy Reau: I think that not all PCPs are the same. For a lot of our Asian patients, their hepatitis B is followed by a primary care provider that has a lot of expertise in hepatitis B, and our primary care doctors do hard things, right? They can give GLP-1s, which are injection therapy. They might be using cholesterol medicines that are alternative options. They might be giving bisphosphonates that are IV. I mean, there's lots of things that PCPs do that are inconvenient. I think a primary care provider that has a large population of hepatitis B might really be excited about another drug, because it affects a majority of the patients. I think PCPs that have a handful of hepatitis B patients, where liver cancer surveillance, and even antiviral therapy may not be done the way that our guidance would suggest, those patients are probably going to have to be linked to a more specialized center. I also think that just the approval of a new drug and a new mechanism of action will have an uptick in screening. One of the biggest issues in the US is the majority of hepatitis B patients are still not diagnosed, and of those that are diagnosed and appropriate for treatment, the majority are not on any treatment. So, hopefully, it'll shift a little more towards screening and standard of care. Tatyana Kushner: Yeah, that is a really good point, and I think that it will depend on the care setting, and whether they have many patients who have hepatitis B, then why not take this on. But again, there’s the question of ALT flares. I mean, I'm scared when I see these high ALT elevations, how about a primary care doctor who is seeing all these hepatitis B carriers, or you know, patients that are relatively well controlled, who hasn't really dealt with ALT flares in this setting. I think it's those kinds of situations that may be a little bit distinct in a specialized setting versus primary care, but I agree that if they have a lot of hepatitis B experience, they may want to be able to be the ones that are offering this to their patients. Location and population
Global, with emphasis on the United States and Asian communities. Affected population: Over 240 million people worldwide with chronic hepatitis B (CHB), including a subgroup of approximately 45% with baseline hepatitis B surface antigen levels of 1000 IU/mL or lower.
Event
Discussion of bepirovirsen, an investigational antisense oligonucleotide positioned as a potential functional cure for chronic hepatitis B (CHB). Expert hepatologists (Nancy Reau, MD; Tatyana Kushner, MD; Kimberly Brown, MD) weighed primary care against specialized centers for delivering this treatment. Response, status and uncertainty
The treatment is under review, with a PDUFA target action date of October 26, 2026. Experts are discussing care delivery models, considering whether treatment should be administered in primary care or specialized centers, particularly for Asian communities. There are concerns about monitoring demands, including surveillance for ALT flares. GSK is actively planning educational efforts. The approval of a new drug with a novel mechanism may lead to an uptick in screening and standard of care. 乙型肝炎与丙型肝炎:哪种病毒性肝脏感染更危险?

Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: Which viral liver infection is more dangerous?
新闻简报本文对乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎这两种常见的病毒性肝脏感染进行了比较分析。它解释说,两者均可导致慢性肝病、肝硬化、肝功能衰竭和肝癌,但在 传播 和疾病进展方面有所不同。乙型肝炎通过血液、受感染的体液传播,可以通过疫苗接种预防,而丙型肝炎主要通过受感染的血液传播,没有疫苗。文章指出,丙型肝炎更常见地导致慢性感染,但这两种感染都可以通过适当的医疗护理来控制。它强调了早期检测和 Z​​XQTERMVQXZ 对于这两种感染以预防严重并发症的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: Which viral liver infection is more dangerous?

News Brief
This article provides a comparative analysis of hepatitis B and hepatitis C, two common viral liver infections. It explains that both can lead to chronic liver disease, cirrhosis, liver failure, and liver cancer, but differ in transmission and disease progression. Hepatitis B is transmitted through blood, infected body fluids, and can be prevented by vaccination, while hepatitis C is mainly transmitted through infected blood and has no vaccine. The article notes that hepatitis C more commonly leads to chronic infection, but both can be managed with appropriate medical care. It emphasizes the importance of early detection and treatment for both infections to prevent severe complications.
Original source text (English)
Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: Which viral liver infection is more dangerous? Hepatitis B Vs Hepatitis C: How Are These Viral Infections Different? Read on to know how different these two viral infections are and what symptoms the body actually shows when it is infected with the virus. Medically Verified By: Dr. Deepak Ganga P Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: Which viral liver infection is more dangerous? Hepatitis B and Hepatitis C are two of the most common viral infections affecting the liver. Both hepatitis can lead to chronic liver disease, cirrhosis, liver failure, and liver cancer. Hepatitis B and hepatitis C have many similarities, which raises the question of which is more harmful. Both infections have serious consequences to health, but they differ in the way of transmission and development of the disease. Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: What Are The Key Differences? In an exclusive interaction with TheHealthSite.com, Dr. Deepak Ganga P, Senior Consultant - Medical Gastroenterology, Hepatology and Interventional Endoscopy, KIMS Hospitals, Mahadevapura, Bengaluru, explained why it is important to understand both types of hepatitis, and how they differ from each other. Hepatitis B is common as hepatitis C and is caused by hepatitis B virus (HBV). It is transmitted when blood and infected body fluids come in contact with people. Transmission can occur from mother to child during childbirth, due to unprotected sex, sharing of needles or unsafe medical procedures. In adults hepatitis B could resolve without treatment. Hepatitis C is an infection leading to by a virus called hepatitis C virus (HCV) and is transmitted mainly through infected blood. Nowadays, the greatest number of people get the infection through contributing blood or sharing needles. Sexual transmission is uncommon, similar to hepatitis B, although it might take place at times. HCV is more commonly transitioned into chronic infection when compared to HBV, which in the majority of cases leads to chronic liver disease and thus to serious conditions in the absence of treatment. Hepatitis B vs Hepatitis C: How Different Are The Symptoms? As for the symptoms of the disease, both infections are silent because many infected people do not present any symptoms for many years. When symptoms occur, fatigue or lack of appetite may appear and nausea, discomfort in the abdomen as well as dark urine and yellow skin may also occur. The question arises as to which is riskier. The hepatitis B vaccine has proven very effective and has played an immense role in reducing infection rates. But even though chronic hepatitis B has not been cured, the use of antiviral treatment can help to hold the virus in check and avoid adverse outcomes. In contrast to hepatitis B, hepatitis C does not have any vaccine, generically making its prevention even more important. Luckily, the progress of science and medicine brought some revolutionary DAAs (direct-acting antivirals), which allow curing hepatitis C in over 95% of cases under the right situations and at the right time. Why Is Treating Hepatitis Important? Still, doctors insist that instead of true comparison of the two types of infection, it is far more important to understand that both can be fatal if left ignored. The detection of either type can be achieved through blood tests thus, people with considerable risk factors need to get tested and cure if necessary. You may like to read Getting a vaccine for hepatitis B, employing safe sex practices, and staying away from sharing needles or personal belongings that may contact blood. Safe medical practices help in lowering the likelihood of infection. With early diagnosis, appropriate medical care, and treatment, it is possible for many hepatitis B or C patients to have otherwise normal and productive lives with low chances of liver complications arising. Do not take this as medical advice, and ensure that you are checked by a proper doctor before encouraging any home remedies for managing the condition. Treat all Hepatitis B cases immediately to save lives | Hindustan Times - Hindustan Times
新闻简报This opinion piece from Hindustan Times highlights the urgent need to treat all Hepatitis B cases in India immediately to save lives. The author, SK Sarin, notes that Hepatitis B caused 1.1 million deaths globally in 2024, with 30-35 million infected in India alone. Despite having a National Viral Hepatitis Control Programme since 2018, India is missing WHO targets with only 3-15% diagnosed and 5% treated. The article advocates adopting new Asian guidelines recommending 'Treat All' instead of 'Treat Select', which would reduce transmission risk, prevent cirrhosis and liver cancer, and be more cost-effective than current approaches. The author calls for physicians to change from being gatekeepers to preventers and for government policies to support broader treatment access.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Treat all Hepatitis B cases immediately to save lives | Hindustan Times - Hindustan Times

News Brief
This opinion piece from Hindustan Times highlights the urgent need to treat all Hepatitis B cases in India immediately to save lives. The author, SK Sarin, notes that Hepatitis B caused 1.1 million deaths globally in 2024, with 30-35 million infected in India alone. Despite having a National Viral Hepatitis Control Programme since 2018, India is missing WHO targets with only 3-15% diagnosed and 5% treated. The article advocates adopting new Asian guidelines recommending 'Treat All' instead of 'Treat Select', which would reduce transmission risk, prevent cirrhosis and liver cancer, and be more cost-effective than current approaches. The author calls for physicians to change from being gatekeepers to preventers and for government policies to support broader treatment access.
Original source text (English)
Treat all Hepatitis B cases immediately to save lives If the Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is suppressed, the risk of transmission to the family and close contacts is negligible. Published on: Jul 28, 2026, 08:36:41 IST By SK Sarin Prefer HT on Google Share via Copy link Two infections had comparable global death tolls in 2024: Tuberculosis (TB) killed 1.23 million, and Hepatitis B 1.1 million, while HIV-related illnesses killed 630,000. For Hepatitis B, the toll was 17% more than it was in 2015. In 2021, Hepatitis B consumed an estimated 21.5 million disability-adjusted life-years (DALYs). Nearly 30-35 million in India suffer from this infection and about 5-10 million may eventually die due to cirrhosis and liver cancer. Yet public recognition, political attention, and funding are strikingly unequal, with Hepatitis B receiving very low quanta of each. No wonder, the world, including India will miss the 2030 WHO targets — instead of the 90% targeted, diagnosing merely 3-15%, and the 80% targeted, treating just 5% of infected. Less than a million are receiving treatment despite a large National Viral Hepatitis Control Programme (NVHCP) since 2018. If the Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is suppressed, the risk of transmission to the family and close contacts is negligible. (Pexel) The problem is the medical profession itself. Unlike HIV and Hepatitis C (another liver virus causing cirrhosis and cancer) where every patient receives treatment, for Hepatitis B , medical algorithms demand high viral load, abnormal liver enzymes, liver scarring or fibrosis for getting treatment. Patients who fail to qualify must undergo repeated blood tests, viral-DNA measurements, fibrosis assessments, specialist consultations throughout life. As the virus remains silent for years, patients are often told to wait. This becomes a painful ordeal. The system spends money monitoring the virus, refusing to suppress it, while hurting the psyche of the infected. These recommendations originate mostly in western countries where tests and drugs are expensive. Also Read: Who should get tested for hepatitis? Gastroenterologist shares screening guidelines even if you have no symptoms A ray of hope, though, came last month. The new Asian guidelines on Hepatitis B, recommend “Treat All” rather than “Treat Select”. The generic antivirals are low-cost in retail and also available under the NHVCP, and should cost less than ₹ 1,000 for a daily pill for a month. Pills taken daily can effectively suppress the virus for life with minimal adverse event and resistance. After China, India houses the largest number of Hepatitis B subjects. India should adopt a “test once, treat early and monitor simply” approach. Broadening treatment would not only reduce the DALY burden but also lower the several-fold higher cost of treatment for cirrhosis, cancer and transplantation for patients. India must adopt the Asian guidelines and reverse the expensive misallocation of scarce health resources in testing to treatment. Ghana showed that using conventional criteria made about only 10% of patients eligible, but by treating anyone with detectable Hepatitis B DNA raised eligibility to 87%. Health systems should stop spending scarce resources proving that people with Hepatitis B are not yet sick enough to treat. India will save substantially by screening households of infected and treating most, and early. Developing its own national cost-effectiveness model, using Indian prices, disease progression, adherence, cancer costs, productivity, and procurement rates would help. If the Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is suppressed, the risk of transmission to the family and close contacts is negligible. One’s own risk of cirrhosis and liver cancer falls markedly. This changes expenditure from high-cost rescue care for a small number of very sick patients to low-cost prevention distributed across a much larger population. We, the doctors, present the hurdle: Physicians must stop being gatekeepers who certify when Hepatitis B has caused enough damage. They should become preventers who ensure it never does. Also Read: Worried about Hepatitis B and C? Gastroenterologist Dr Shubham Vatsya explains facts that every family should know We need to do a lot to reduce the stigma of Hepatitis B, very often associated with HIV. We need to join the EMPATHY (Empowering People Against Hepatitis) campaign. Physicians should explicitly state in certificates and counselling that ordinary social and workplace contact does not spread HBV. They should challenge inappropriate employment exclusion, refusal of procedures, and marriage-related misinformation. Treating the infected should be strongly supplemented by protecting the next generation. HBV vaccine given at birth and followed by week 6, 10 and 14 can protect nearly every newborn. India has taken good strides in this direction, but much more can be done. Every adult should also get this most economical and effective vaccine. July 28 is World Hepatitis Day; this time, it is being observed with the theme “Hepatitis: Let us break it down”. We need to break the barriers, starting with social acceptance and dignity for the infected, change in attitude of the physicians to “Treat Most”, and supportive policies of treatment and funding by the government. Every single Hepatitis B death is a reminder of our inept policies and delayed judgement. Act now, and, collectively to eliminate hepatitis B. SK Sarin is professor of eminence, Institute of Liver and Biliary Sciences, New Delhi. 调查显示人们对 慢性乙型肝炎 与肝癌的联系认识不足 - HCPLive

Survey Reveals Low Awareness of Chronic Hepatitis B’s Link to Liver Cancer - HCPLive
新闻简报A survey conducted by GSK and Ipsos for World Hepatitis Day revealed significant gaps in public awareness of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) and its association with liver cancer. The findings, based on responses from approximately 5000 adults across Japan, Taiwan, China, Germany, and Canada, showed that nearly 90% of Canadians had little to no awareness of CHB, with only 31% perceiving it as a high cancer risk. Despite affecting an estimated 262,000 people in Canada, only 14% had been tested for CHB. Globally, CHB is responsible for about 56% of liver cancer cases, impacting over 240 million individuals. Cary James of the World Hepatitis Alliance emphasized the need for improved awareness and understanding of CHB, noting that stigma and lack of access to testing are major barriers. The survey also found that awareness of the link between hepatitis B and liver cancer could motivate testing, with three-quarters of respondents indicating they would be more likely to get tested if informed. Public health organizations continue efforts to meet the World Health Organization’s goal of eliminating viral hepatitis as a public health threat by 2030.

时间
该调查是在 2026 年 7 月 28 日世界肝炎日之前进行的,并于 2026 年 7 月 28 日发布。
地点与对象
该调查涵盖日本、台湾、中国、德国和加拿大约 5000 名成年人,其中 1061 名加拿大受访者年龄≥ 18 岁。
事件
GSK 和 Ipsos 进行的一项调查显示,公众对 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 及其与肝癌的联系的认识较低,世界肝炎联盟的 Cary James 讨论了调查结果。
规模、影响与风险
The survey found that only 31% of Canadian respondents considered CHB a high cancer risk, and only 14% had been tested for CHB, despite it affecting an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. Globally, CHB accounts for approximately 56% of liver cancer cases and affects more than 240 million people.
应对、状态与不确定性
该调查凸显了公众对慢性乙型肝炎的理解和认识方面的差距,对检测、预防和 治疗 产生影响。公共卫生组织正在努力实现世卫组织到 2030 年消除病毒性肝炎的目标。
